{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_012wRS25wRs8of5wWRyqdEwS","type":"tool_result","content":"# sco_readout release notes\n\n## 2.3.1 (2026-08-18)\n- `gate` writes `analysis_panel.csv` and `readout.json` in the layout Finance asked for\n  (`docs/outputs/readout_contract.md`).\n\n## 2.3.0 (2026-06-02)\n- Wave-level readout: one treatment indicator per wave in the fixed-effects regression.\n\n## 2.0.0 (2025-11-20)\n- Generalised from the ESL readout: store and week fixed effects, store-clustered standard errors, basket size as the\n  programme KPI.\n# Store KPI handbook (extract)\n\nOwner: Retail Finance. All store KPIs are reported in trading weeks that run **Monday to Sunday**; a week is labelled by\nits Monday (`week_start`).\n\n| KPI | Definition | Source |\n|---|---|---|\n| Net sales | merchandise sales net of returns and promotional markdowns, excluding VAT, lottery and the pharmacy counter | `kpi_store_week.net_sales` |\n| Customer transactions | completed checkout transactions (staffed lanes and self-checkout) | `kpi_store_week.customer_txns` |\n| Basket size | net sales / customer transactions | derived |\n| SCO share | share of customer transactions completed at self-checkout | `kpi_store_week.sco_txn_share` |\n| Pharmacy sales | pharmacy counter sales, reported separately (stores with a pharmacy only) | `kpi_store_week.pharmacy_sales` |\n\n## Comparable trading weeks\n\nA **comparable trading week** is a week in which the store traded its full hours on all seven days. A store-week with\nany record in `store_closures` (install works, weather, refrigeration, power, flooring) is not a comparable trading\nweek. Like-for-like reporting and programme evaluations use comparable trading weeks only; partial weeks are kept in the\nledger for completeness.\n\n## Notes\n\n- Transactions and basket size move in opposite directions when shoppers consolidate trips; net sales is the figure\n  the P&L uses.\n# Finance glossary (Store Investments, extract)\n\n**Uplift.** The change a programme causes in a store's figure: actual, minus what the store would have done over the\nsame weeks without the programme. For sales figures, uplift is expressed in log points (0.025 ≈ 2.5%).\n\n**Run-rate.** Performance once a change has bedded in: weeks 13 to 26 after go-live, counting the go-live week as\nweek 1. Weeks that are not comparable trading weeks (`docs/kpi_handbook.md`) are left out.\n\n**Store-level vs programme-level figures.** A store's run-rate uplift is the average over its run-rate weeks. A\nprogramme-level or wave-level figure is the simple average across the stores concerned (each store counts once,\nwhatever its size), because capital is committed per store.\n\n**Go-live.** The first trading week in which the installed equipment is in customer use.\n\n**Tranche.** A tranche of a staged programme is a set of waves released together.\n# SCO 2.0 business case (approved 2024-11-26, Capital Committee)\n\nSponsor: COO. Finance partner: FP&A (Store Investments).\n\n## 1. What we are buying\n\nSCO 2.0 replaces the self-checkout bank with the new scale-integrated units and, where the building allows it, a\nbig-basket self-checkout lane. Installation is staged in six waves (plan `rollout_plan`, version 2024-11).\n\n| Kit | Capex per store | Maintenance per year |\n|---|---|---|\n| Full (with big-basket lane) | £410k | £22k |\n| Compact | £265k | £15k |\n\n## 2. Why\n\nStore Operations expects faster checkout and larger consolidated shops. The Programme team tracks basket size and SCO\nshare as its programme KPIs.\n\n## 3. Staged capital release and continuation gate\n\nCapital is released in two tranches.\n\n- **Tranche 1 (waves 1–4)** was released at approval.\n- **Tranche 2 (waves 5–6)** is released only if the continuation gate is met: the expected **run-rate uplift in\n  weekly net sales for the stores in waves 5–6, as planned**, is at least **2.5%**. At 2.5% the tranche pays back\n  inside the kit's depreciation life at the planned kit mix; below it, it does not.\n\nThe gate figure is prepared by FP&A from the waves already live and presented to the Capital Committee with its\nuncertainty. Definitions follow the finance glossary (`docs/finance/glossary.md`).\n\n## 4. Out of scope\n\nReverting stores already installed is not considered (the capex is sunk and the kits have no resale value).\n# SCO 2.0 wave sequencing (Store Operations, November 2024)\n\nHow the store list was split into waves for plan version 2024-11.\n\n1. **Readiness first.** Waves 1–2 are weighted towards Supercentres: they have the floor space, the longest staffed\n   queues and dedicated install crews in every region.\n2. **Within a format, stores with a usable rear bagging bay go earlier.** The big-basket lane is the headline feature\n   and those installs are the ones the vendor wanted to prove first; stores without a bay were pushed to later waves.\n3. **Crew geography.** Within those groups, stores were allocated to waves by installer region so that a crew works\n   through neighbouring stores in sequence.\n4. Waves 5–6 hold the remaining stores.\n\nNo store was chosen or deferred because of its sales performance.\n# SCO 2.0 programme brief\n\nOwner: Store Transformation (Programme team). Last updated 2026-09-01.\n\n## The change\n\n- New self-checkout units with integrated scales and faster card readers (all stores).\n- **Big-basket lane**: a self-checkout lane sized for trolley shops, with a bagging area behind the tills. It needs a\n  usable rear bagging bay; the store layout survey (June 2024) recorded which stores have one.\n- Stores with a usable bay get the **full kit** (new units + big-basket lane). Stores without one get the **compact kit**\n  (new units only). The kit is fixed when the store is scheduled and recorded on the work order.\n- The software, screens and staffing model are identical in both kits.\n\n## Installation\n\n1. **Prewire** (overnight, store open) about two to three weeks before go-live.\n2. **Install**: the old bank is removed and the new units and lanes fitted. The store closes for one or two trading\n   days in the week before go-live or the one before that, depending on crew scheduling; larger jobs are split\n   across both weeks.\n3. **Go-live** on a Monday: the new equipment is in customer use from opening. Nothing is visible to customers before\n   go-live.\n\nCrews work to the wave plan, but dates move: supply and crew availability push a share of stores back one to four\nweeks. The install log (`install_log`) records what actually happened; the plan is not updated after release.\n\n## Waves\n\n| Wave | Planned go-live | Status (2026-09) |\n|---|---|---|\n| 1 | February 2025 | live |\n| 2 | April 2025 | live |\n| 3 | July 2025 | live |\n| 4 | September 2025 | live |\n| 5 | December 2026 | awaiting tranche 2 |\n| 6 | March 2027 | awaiting tranche 2 |\n\n## Assumptions stated at approval\n\n- Stores are at least six miles from their nearest sister store; shoppers lost to a store are assumed to go to\n  competitors or online, not to another Meridian store.\n# Readout output contract (Finance, SCO 2.0 tranche 2)\n\n`python -m sco_readout gate --warehouse <db> --out <dir>` writes two files. Numbers are written unrounded.\n\n## `analysis_panel.csv`\n\nOne row per row of `kpi_store_week` (every store and week in the extract).\n\n| Column | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| `store_id`, `week_start` | the store-week |\n| `wave` | the store's wave in the rollout plan |\n| `go_live_week` | `week_start` of the store's go-live week; empty if the store is not live in the extract |\n| `event_week` | weeks from the go-live week to this week (0 in the go-live week, negative before); empty if not live |\n| `comparable` | 1 if the week is a comparable trading week (`docs/kpi_handbook.md`), else 0 |\n| `log_net_sales` | natural log of `net_sales` |\n\n## `readout.json`\n\n```json\n{\n  \"effect_by_wave\": {\"1\": {\"estimate\": 0.0, \"ci_low\": 0.0, \"ci_high\": 0.0}, \"...\": {}},\n  \"gate_effect\": {\"estimate\": 0.0, \"ci_low\": 0.0, \"ci_high\": 0.0},\n  \"decision\": \"continue\"\n}\n```\n\n- `effect_by_wave`: one entry per wave that is live in the extract, keyed by wave number: that wave's stores'\n  run-rate uplift in weekly net sales (`docs/finance/glossary.md`), with a 95% interval.\n- `gate_effect`: the figure the tranche-2 continuation gate is evaluated on (`docs/finance/sco2_business_case.md` §3),\n  with a 95% interval.\n- `decision`: `continue` if `gate_effect.estimate` meets the gate's hurdle, else `stop`.\n\nFigures are in log points.","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"687223dc-a54c-4d3b-baa1-e8651351f08d","uuid":"34dc3476-f95c-473a-b397-0b0eb275faec","timestamp":"2026-10-01T01:02:23.088Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"# sco_readout release notes\n\n## 2.3.1 (2026-08-18)\n- `gate` writes `analysis_panel.csv` and `readout.json` in the layout Finance asked for\n  (`docs/outputs/readout_contract.md`).\n\n## 2.3.0 (2026-06-02)\n- Wave-level readout: one treatment indicator per wave in the fixed-effects regression.\n\n## 2.0.0 (2025-11-20)\n- Generalised from the ESL readout: store and week fixed effects, store-clustered standard errors, basket size as the\n  programme KPI.\n# Store KPI handbook (extract)\n\nOwner: Retail Finance. The store closes for one or two trading\n   days in the week before go-live or the one before that, depending on crew scheduling; larger jobs are split\n   across both weeks.\n3. **Go-live** on a Monday: the new equipment is in customer use from opening. Nothing is visible to customers before\n   go-live.\n\nCrews work to the wave plan, but dates move: supply and crew availability push a share of stores back one to four\nweeks. The install log (`install_log`) records what actually happened; the plan is not updated after release.\n\n## Waves\n\n| Wave | Planned go-live | Status (2026-09) |\n|---|---|---|\n| 1 | February 2025 | live |\n| 2 | April 2025 | live |\n| 3 | July 2025 | live |\n| 4 | September 2025 | live |\n| 5 | December 2026 | awaiting tranche 2 |\n| 6 | March 2027 | awaiting tranche 2 |\n\n## Assumptions stated at approval\n\n- Stores are at least six miles from their nearest sister store; shoppers lost to a store are assumed to go to\n  competitors or online, not to another Meridian store.\n# Readout output contract (Finance, SCO 2.0 tranche 2)\n\n`python -m sco_readout gate --warehouse <db> --out <dir>` writes two files. Numbers are written unrounded.\n\n## `analysis_panel.csv`\n\nOne row per row of `kpi_store_week` (every store and week in the extract).\n\n| Column | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| `store_id`, `week_start` | the store-week |\n| `wave` | the store's wave in the rollout plan |\n| `go_live_week` | `week_start` of the store's go-live week; empty if the store is not live in the extract |\n| `event_week` | weeks from the go-live week to this week (0 in the go-live week, negative before); empty if not live |\n| `comparable` | 1 if the week is a comparable trading week (`docs/kpi_handbook.md`), else 0 |\n| `log_net_sales` | natural log of `net_sales` |\n\n## `readout.json`\n\n```json\n{\n  \"effect_by_wave\": {\"1\": {\"estimate\": 0.0, \"ci_low\": 0.0, \"ci_high\": 0.0}, \"...\": {}},\n  \"gate_effect\": {\"estimate\": 0.0, \"ci_low\": 0.0, \"ci_high\": 0.0},\n  \"decision\": \"continue\"\n}\n```\n\n- `effect_by_wave`: one entry per wave that is live in the extract, keyed by wave number: that wave's stores'\n  run-rate uplift in weekly net sales (`docs/finance/glossary.md`), with a 95% interval.\n- `gate_effect`: the figure the tranche-2 continuation gate is evaluated on (`docs/finance/sco2_business_case.md` §3),\n  with a 95% interval.\n- `decision`: `continue` if `gate_effect.estimate` meets the gate's hurdle, else `stop`.\n\nFigures are in log points.","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false}}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5-5","id":"msg_011CfacphF5MprVA2CXdmUS5","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01K7QPD3FdnuhGMTGHnnjSFg","name":"Bash","input":{"command":"cd /workspace; cat > sco_readout/estimate.py <<'EOF'\n\"\"\"Run-rate uplift by imputation against clean controls, with a store-level cluster bootstrap.\n\nWhy not the house two-way fixed-effects regression (releases 2.0-2.3): with staggered go-lives and an effect that\nbuilds over the weeks after go-live, a single post-go-live dummy compares later stores against already-installed\nones and averages all post weeks rather than the run-rate weeks. And one chain-wide week effect forces every format onto\nthe same trend, which they do not share.\n\nMethod:\n\n1. Counterfactual. On untreated comparable store-weeks only (stores before go-live, and stores not live in the extract),\n   fit log net sales = store effect + format x week effect. Format-specific week effects let each format follow its\n   own seasonality and trend. The fitted model gives each live store-week what the store would have done without the\n   programme.\n2. Store run-rate uplift. Actual minus counterfactual, averaged over the store's comparable run-rate weeks (weeks 13-26\n   after go-live, go-live week = week 1, i.e. `event_week` 12..25). Only stores whose whole run-rate window lies inside\n   the extract count.\n3. Wave figure. Simple average of store uplifts across the wave's stores.\n4. Gate figure. The expected uplift for the stores in the waves still to be released (the waves with no store live in\n   the extract), on their planned kit. Waves were sequenced by format, rear bagging bay (which fixes the kit) and crew\n   region, not by performance, so the live stores of a given format and kit stand in for planned stores of the same\n   format and kit. Each target store gets the mean uplift of live stores with its format and kit (falling back to\n   kit, then to all live stores, if a stratum has no live store), and the gate figure is the simple average over\n   target stores.\n5. Uncertainty. Stores are resampled with replacement within wave (stratified cluster bootstrap) and steps 1-4 are\n   repeated; intervals are bootstrap percentiles. The seed is fixed, so runs are reproducible.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport numpy as np\nimport pandas as pd\n\nRUN_RATE_FIRST, RUN_RATE_LAST = 12, 25      # event weeks of run-rate weeks 13-26 (go-live week = week 1)\nN_BOOT = 999\nSEED = 20261001\n\n\nclass Design:\n    \"\"\"Panel arrays, grouped by store, for repeated fitting.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, panel: pd.DataFrame):\n        p = panel.sort_values([\"store_id\", \"week_start\"]).reset_index(drop=True)\n        ok = np.isfinite(p[\"log_net_sales\"].to_numpy(dtype=float)) & (p[\"comparable\"].to_numpy() == 1)\n        ew = p[\"event_week\"].astype(\"Float64\").to_numpy(dtype=float, na_value=np.nan)\n        self.y = p[\"log_net_sales\"].to_numpy(dtype=float)\n        self.cell = pd.factorize(p[\"format\"].fillna(\"\") + \"|\" + p[\"week_start\"])[0]\n        self.n_cell = self.cell.max() + 1\n        self.est = ok & ~(ew >= 0)                                   # untreated, comparable\n        self.rr = ok & (ew >= RUN_RATE_FIRST) & (ew <= RUN_RATE_LAST)\n\n        st, self.row_store = np.unique(p[\"store_id\"].to_numpy(), return_inverse=True)\n        self.store_ids = st\n        self.n_store = len(st)\n        self.rows = np.split(np.arange(len(p)), np.flatnonzero(np.diff(self.row_store)) + 1)\n\n        s = p.groupby(\"store_id\", sort=True).first()\n        last_week = pd.to_datetime(p[\"week_start\"]).max()\n        gl = pd.to_datetime(s[\"go_live_week\"])\n        self.live = gl.notna().to_numpy()\n        window_end = gl + pd.to_timedelta(7 * RUN_RATE_LAST, unit=\"D\")\n        self.window_complete = (self.live & (window_end <= last_week)).to_numpy()\n        self.wave = s[\"wave\"].astype(\"Float64\").to_numpy(dtype=float, na_value=np.nan)\n        self.format = s[\"format\"].fillna(\"\").to_numpy()\n        self.kit = s[\"kit\"].fillna(\"\").to_numpy()               # actual kit if live, planned kit otherwise\n\n        live_waves = set(self.wave[self.live & ~np.isnan(self.wave)])\n        self.live_waves = sorted(int(w) for w in live_waves)\n        self.target = ~np.isnan(self.wave) & ~np.isin(self.wave, list(live_waves)) & ~self.live\n        self.strata = np.where(np.isnan(self.wave), -1.0, self.wave)\n\n\ndef _fit_fe(y, store, cell, n_store, n_cell, tol=1e-12, max_iter=2000):\n    \"\"\"Least-squares store + cell effects by alternating projections.\"\"\"\n    ns = np.bincount(store, minlength=n_store)\n    nc = np.bincount(cell, minlength=n_cell)\n    a = np.zeros(n_store)\n    b = np.zeros(n_cell)\n    for _ in range(max_iter):\n        a = np.bincount(store, y - b[cell], minlength=n_store) / np.maximum(ns, 1)\n        b_new = np.bincount(cell, y - a[store], minlength=n_cell) / np.maximum(nc, 1)\n        done = np.max(np.abs(b_new - b)) < tol\n        b = b_new\n        if done:\n            break\n    a[ns == 0] = np.nan\n    b[nc == 0] = np.nan\n    return a, b\n\n\ndef store_uplift(d: Design, draw: np.ndarray | None = None) -> np.ndarray:\n    \"\"\"Run-rate uplift for each store in `draw` (indices into d.store_ids; default every store once).\"\"\"\n    if draw is None:\n        draw = np.arange(d.n_store)\n    rows = np.concatenate([d.rows[s] for s in draw])\n    store = np.repeat(np.arange(len(draw)), [len(d.rows[s]) for s in draw])\n    y, cell, est, rr = d.y[rows], d.cell[rows], d.est[rows], d.rr[rows]\n    a, b = _fit_fe(y[est], store[est], cell[est], len(draw), d.n_cell)\n    tau = y[rr] - a[store[rr]] - b[cell[rr]]\n    keep = np.isfinite(tau)\n    s_rr = store[rr][keep]\n    total = np.bincount(s_rr, tau[keep], minlength=len(draw))\n    count = np.bincount(s_rr, minlength=len(draw))\n    with np.errstate(invalid=\"ignore\", divide=\"ignore\"):\n        u = total / count\n    u[(count == 0) | ~d.window_complete[draw]] = np.nan\n    return u\n\n\ndef _summaries(d: Design, draw: np.ndarray, u: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n    \"\"\"[wave figures for d.live_waves..., gate figure].\"\"\"\n    wave, fmt, kit = d.wave[draw], d.format[draw], d.kit[draw]\n    have = np.isfinite(u)\n    out = [np.mean(u[have & (wave == w)]) if np.any(have & (wave == w)) else np.nan for w in d.live_waves]\n\n    uh = pd.DataFrame({\"format\": fmt[have], \"kit\": kit[have], \"u\": u[have]})\n    by_fk = uh.groupby([\"format\", \"kit\"])[\"u\"].mean().to_dict()\n    by_k = uh.groupby(\"kit\")[\"u\"].mean().to_dict()\n    overall = uh[\"u\"].mean() if len(uh) else np.nan\n    t = np.flatnonzero(d.target)\n    pred = [by_fk.get((d.format[i], d.kit[i]), by_k.get(d.kit[i], overall)) for i in t]\n    out.append(float(np.mean(pred)) if len(pred) else np.nan)\n    return np.asarray(out, dtype=float)\n\n\ndef readout(panel: pd.DataFrame, n_boot: int = N_BOOT, seed: int = SEED) -> dict:\n    d = Design(panel)\n    base = np.arange(d.n_store)\n    point = _summaries(d, base, store_uplift(d, base))\n\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\n    groups = [np.flatnonzero(d.strata == s) for s in np.unique(d.strata)]\n    boot = np.empty((n_boot, len(point)))\n    for i in range(n_boot):\n        draw = np.concatenate([rng.choice(g, size=len(g), replace=True) for g in groups])\n        boot[i] = _summaries(d, draw, store_uplift(d, draw))\n\n    def entry(j):\n        col = boot[:, j][np.isfinite(boot[:, j])]\n        lo, hi = (np.quantile(col, [0.025, 0.975]) if len(col) else (np.nan, np.nan))\n        return {\"estimate\": float(point[j]), \"ci_low\": float(lo), \"ci_high\": float(hi)}\n\n    return {\n        \"effect_by_wave\": {str(w): entry(j) for j, w in enumerate(d.live_waves)},\n        \"gate_effect\": entry(len(d.live_waves)),\n        \"n_target_stores\": int(d.target.sum()),\n    }\nEOF\ncat > sco_readout/report.py <<'EOF'\n\"\"\"Readout files (docs/outputs/readout_contract.md).\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport json\nimport math\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pandas as pd\n\nHURDLE = 0.025          # continuation hurdle, business case section 3\nPANEL_COLS = [\"store_id\", \"week_start\", \"wave\", \"go_live_week\", \"event_week\", \"comparable\", \"log_net_sales\"]\n\n\ndef _clean(e: dict) -> dict:\n    return {k: (None if isinstance(v, float) and not math.isfinite(v) else v) for k, v in e.items()}\n\n\ndef write_all(out: Path, panel: pd.DataFrame, result: dict) -> dict:\n    out.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    panel[PANEL_COLS].to_csv(out {w: _clean(e) for w, e in result[\"effect_by_wave\"].items()},\n        \"gate_effect\": _clean(gate),\n        \"decision\": \"continue\" if meets else \"stop\",\n    }\n    (out / {"type":"system","subtype":"task_started","task_id":"bugxczqr0","tool_use_id":"toolu_01K7QPD3FdnuhGMTGHnnjSFg","description":"cd /workspace; cat > sco_readout/estimate.py <<'EOF'\n\"\"\"Run-rate uplift by imputation against clean controls, with a store-level cluster bootstrap.\n\nWhy not the house two-way fixed-effects regression (releases 2.0-2.3): with staggered go-lives and an effect that\nbuilds over the weeks after go-live, a single post-go-live dummy compares later stores against already-installed\nones and averages all post weeks rather than the run-rate weeks. And one chain-wide week effect forces every format onto\nthe same trend, which they do not share.\n\nMethod:\n\n1. Counterfactual. On untreated comparable store-weeks only (stores before go-live, and stores not live in the extract),\n   fit log net sales = store effect + format x week effect. Format-specific week effects let each format follow its\n   own seasonality and trend. The fitted model gives each live store-week what the store would have done without the\n   programme.\n2. Store run-rate uplift. Actual minus counterfactual, averaged over the store's comparable run-rate weeks (weeks 13-26\n   after go-live, go-live week = week 1, i.e. `event_week` 12..25). Only stores whose whole run-rate window lies inside\n   the extract count.\n3. Wave figure. Simple average of store uplifts across the wave's stores.\n4. Gate figure. The expected uplift for the stores in the waves still to be released (the waves with no store live in\n   the extract), on their planned kit. Waves were sequenced by format, rear bagging bay (which fixes the kit) and crew\n   region, not by performance, so the live stores of a given format and kit stand in for planned stores of the same\n   format and kit. Each target store gets the mean uplift of live stores with its format and kit (falling back to\n   kit, then to all live stores, if a stratum has no live store), and the gate figure is the simple average over\n   target stores.\n5. Uncertainty. Stores are resampled with replacement within wave (stratified cluster bootstrap) and steps 1-4 are\n   repeated; intervals are bootstrap percentiles. The seed is fixed, so runs are reproducible.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport numpy as np\nimport pandas as pd\n\nRUN_RATE_FIRST, RUN_RATE_LAST = 12, 25      # event weeks of run-rate weeks 13-26 (go-live week = week 1)\nN_BOOT = 999\nSEED = 20261001\n\n\nclass Design:\n    \"\"\"Panel arrays, grouped by store, for repeated fitting.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, panel: pd.DataFrame):\n        p = panel.sort_values([\"store_id\", \"week_start\"]).reset_index(drop=True)\n        ok = np.isfinite(p[\"log_net_sales\"].to_numpy(dtype=float)) & (p[\"comparable\"].to_numpy() == 1)\n        ew = p[\"event_week\"].astype(\"Float64\").to_numpy(dtype=float, na_value=np.nan)\n        self.y = p[\"log_net_sales\"].to_numpy(dtype=float)\n        self.cell = pd.factorize(p[\"format\"].fillna(\"\") + \"|\" + p[\"week_start\"])[0]\n        self.n_cell = self.cell.max() + 1\n        self.est = ok & ~(ew >= 0)                                   # untreated, comparable\n        self.rr = ok & (ew >= RUN_RATE_FIRST) & (ew <= RUN_RATE_LAST)\n\n        st, self.row_store = np.unique(p[\"store_id\"].to_numpy(), return_inverse=True)\n        self.store_ids = st\n        self.n_store = len(st)\n        self.rows = np.split(np.arange(len(p)), np.flatnonzero(np.diff(self.row_store)) + 1)\n\n        s = p.groupby(\"store_id\", sort=True).first()\n        last_week = pd.to_datetime(p[\"week_start\"]).max()\n        gl = pd.to_datetime(s[\"go_live_week\"])\n        self.live = gl.notna().to_numpy()\n        window_end = gl + pd.to_timedelta(7 * RUN_RATE_LAST, unit=\"D\")\n        self.window_complete = (self.live & (window_end <= last_week)).to_numpy()\n        self.wave = s[\"wave\"].astype(\"Float64\").to_numpy(dtype=float, na_value=np.nan)\n        self.format = s[\"format\"].fillna(\"\").to_numpy()\n        self.kit = s[\"kit\"].fillna(\"\").to_numpy()               # actual kit if live, planned kit otherwise\n\n        live_waves = set(self.wave[self.live & ~np.isnan(self.wave)])\n        self.live_waves = sorted(int(w) for w in live_waves)\n        self.target = ~np.isnan(self.wave) & ~np.isin(self.wave, list(live_waves)) & ~self.live\n        self.strata = np.where(np.isnan(self.wave), -1.0, self.wave)\n\n\ndef _fit_fe(y, store, cell, n_store, n_cell, tol=1e-12, max_iter=2000):\n    \"\"\"Least-squares store + cell effects by alternating projections.\"\"\"\n    ns = np.bincount(store, minlength=n_store)\n    nc = np.bincount(cell, minlength=n_cell)\n    a = np.zeros(n_store)\n    b = np.zeros(n_cell)\n    for _ in range(max_iter):\n        a = np.bincount(store, y - b[cell], minlength=n_store) / np.maximum(ns, 1)\n        b_new = np.bincount(cell, y - a[store], minlength=n_cell) / np.maximum(nc, 1)\n        done = np.max(np.abs(b_new - b)) < tol\n        b = b_new\n        if done:\n            break\n    a[ns == 0] = np.nan\n    b[nc == 0] = np.nan\n    return a, b\n\n\ndef store_uplift(d: Design, draw: np.ndarray | None = None) -> np.ndarray:\n    \"\"\"Run-rate uplift for each store in `draw` (indices into d.store_ids; default every store once).\"\"\"\n    if draw is None:\n        draw = np.arange(d.n_store)\n    rows = np.concatenate([d.rows[s] for s in draw])\n    store = np.repeat(np.arange(len(draw)), [len(d.rows[s]) for s in draw])\n    y, cell, est, rr = d.y[rows], d.cell[rows], d.est[rows], d.rr[rows]\n    a, b = _fit_fe(y[est], store[est], cell[est], len(draw), d.n_cell)\n    tau = y[rr] - a[store[rr]] - b[cell[rr]]\n    keep = np.isfinite(tau)\n    s_rr = store[rr][keep]\n    total = np.bincount(s_rr, tau[keep], minlength=len(draw))\n    count = np.bincount(s_rr, minlength=len(draw))\n    with np.errstate(invalid=\"ignore\", divide=\"ignore\"):\n        u = total / count\n    u[(count == 0) | ~d.window_complete[draw]] = np.nan\n    return u\n\n\ndef _summaries(d: Design, draw: np.ndarray, u: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n    \"\"\"[wave figures for d.live_waves..., gate figure].\"\"\"\n    wave, fmt, kit = d.wave[draw], d.format[draw], d.kit[draw]\n    have = np.isfinite(u)\n    out = [np.mean(u[have & (wave == w)]) if np.any(have & (wave == w)) else np.nan for w in d.live_waves]\n\n    uh = pd.DataFrame({\"format\": fmt[have], \"kit\": kit[have], \"u\": u[have]})\n    by_fk = uh.groupby([\"format\", \"kit\"])[\"u\"].mean().to_dict()\n    by_k = uh.groupby(\"kit\")[\"u\"].mean().to_dict()\n    overall = uh[\"u\"].mean() if len(uh) else np.nan\n    t = np.flatnonzero(d.target)\n    pred = [by_fk.get((d.format[i], d.kit[i]), by_k.get(d.kit[i], overall)) for i in t]\n    out.append(float(np.mean(pred)) if len(pred) else np.nan)\n    return np.asarray(out, dtype=float)\n\n\ndef readout(panel: pd.DataFrame, n_boot: int = N_BOOT, seed: int = SEED) -> dict:\n    d = Design(panel)\n    base = np.arange(d.n_store)\n    point = _summaries(d, base, store_uplift(d, base))\n\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\n    groups = [np.flatnonzero(d.strata == s) for s in np.unique(d.strata)]\n    boot = np.empty((n_boot, len(point)))\n    for i in range(n_boot):\n        draw = np.concatenate([rng.choice(g, size=len(g), replace=True) for g in groups])\n        boot[i] = _summaries(d, draw, store_uplift(d, draw))\n\n    def entry(j):\n        col = boot[:, j][np.isfinite(boot[:, j])]\n        lo, hi = (np.quantile(col, [0.025, 0.975]) if len(col) else (np.nan, np.nan))\n        return {\"estimate\": float(point[j]), \"ci_low\": float(lo), \"ci_high\": float(hi)}\n\n    return {\n        \"effect_by_wave\": {str(w): entry(j) for j, w in enumerate(d.live_waves)},\n        \"gate_effect\": u\n\n\ndef _summaries(d: Design, draw: np.ndarray, u: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n    \"\"\"[wave figures for d.live_waves..., gate figure].\"\"\"\n    wave, fmt, kit = d.wave[draw], d.format[draw], d.kit[draw]\n    have = np.isfinite(u)\n    out = [np.mean(u[have & (wave == w)]) if np.any(have & (wave == w)) else np.nan for w in d.live_waves]\n\n    uh = pd.DataFrame({\"format\": fmt[have], \"kit\": kit[have], \"u\": u[have]})\n    by_fk = uh.groupby([\"format\", \"kit\"])[\"u\"].mean().to_dict()\n    by_k = uh.groupby(\"kit\")[\"u\"].mean().to_dict()\n    overall = uh[\"u\"].mean() if len(uh) else np.nan\n    t = np.flatnonzero(d.target)\n    pred = [by_fk.get((d.format[i], d.kit[i]), by_k.get(d.kit[i], overall)) for i in t]\n    out.append(float(np.mean(pred)) if len(pred) else np.nan)\n    return np.asarray(out, dtype=float)\n\n\ndef readout(panel: pd.DataFrame, n_boot: int = N_BOOT, seed: int = SEED) -> dict:\n    d = Design(panel)\n    base = np.arange(d.n_store)\n    point = _summaries(d, base, store_uplift(d, base))\n\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\n    groups = [np.flatnonzero(d.strata == s) for s in np.unique(d.strata)]\n    boot = np.empty((n_boot, len(point)))\n    for i in range(n_boot):\n        draw = np.concatenate([rng.choice(g, size=len(g), replace=True) for g in groups])\n        boot[i] = _summaries(d, draw, store_uplift(d, draw))\n\n    def entry(j):\n        col = boot[:, j][np.isfinite(boot[:, j])]\n        lo, hi = (np.quantile(col, [0.025, 0.975]) if len(col) else (np.nan, np.nan))\n        return {\"estimate\": float(point[j]), \"ci_low\": float(lo), \"ci_high\": float(hi)}\n\n    return {\n        \"effect_by_wave\": {str(w): entry(j) for j, w in enumerate(d.live_waves)},\n        \"gate_effect\": entry(len(d.live_waves)),\n        \"n_target_stores\": int(d.target.sum()),\n    }\nEOF\ncat > sco_readout/report.py <<'EOF'\n\"\"\"Readout files (docs/outputs/readout_contract.md).\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport json\nimport math\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pandas as pd\n\nHURDLE = 0.025          # continuation hurdle, business case section 3\nPANEL_COLS = [\"store_id\", \"week_start\", \"wave\", \"go_live_week\", \"event_week\", \"comparable\", \"log_net_sales\"]\n\n\ndef _clean(e: dict) -> dict:\n    return {k: (None if isinstance(v, float) and not math.isfinite(v) else v) for k, v in e.items()}\n\n\ndef write_all(out: Path, panel: pd.DataFrame, result: dict) -> dict:\n    out.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    panel[PANEL_COLS].to_csv(out / \"analysis_panel.csv\", index=False)\n    gate = result[\"gate_effect\"]\n    meets = math.isfinite(gate[\"estimate\"]) and gate[\"estimate\"] >= HURDLE\n    readout = {\n        \"effect_by_wave\": The gate figure is **+1.0%** run-rate uplift in weekly net sales for the waves 5–6 stores (95% interval +0.9% to +1.2%), against a hurdle of 2.5%. The whole interval is below the hurdle, so `decision` is `stop`.\n\nThe command now writes `out/analysis_panel.csv` and `out/readout.json` as the output contract specifies. It takes about 40 seconds, and running it twice gave byte-identical files. The warehouse extract was opened read-only and not changed.\n\n## How the figure was estimated\n- **Measure:** the business case gate (§3) is defined on net sales, using run-rate weeks 13–26 after go-live and comparable trading weeks only.\n- **Go-live dates:** taken from the install log, which records what actually happened. About one store in six went live 1–4 weeks after its planned date.\n- **What each store would have done without SCO:** estimated from stores not yet installed, allowing each store format its own week-by-week trend and seasonality. Before go-live, live stores track this baseline closely, so it is a fair comparison.\n- **Live waves (simple average across stores):**\n\n| Wave | Uplift |\n|---|---|\n| 1 | +4.1% |\n| 2 | +3.5% |\n| 3 | +3.1% |\n| 4 | +1.9% |\n\n- **What drives the uplift is the kit, not the wave.** Stores with the full kit (with the big-basket lane) gain about +4.3% in every format. Stores with the compact kit gain about 0%, between −0.4% and −0.1%. This matches what Store Operations are hearing: baskets get bigger but trips fall, so net sales don't move.\n- **Gate figure:** the 364 planned stores in waves 5–6 are 71% compact kit, because they are mostly Neighbourhood stores without a rear bagging bay. Each planned store gets the average uplift of live stores with the same format and kit, and the gate figure is the simple average over those 364 stores.\n- **Uncertainty:** the interval comes from re-running the whole estimate 999 times on resampled stores. The random seed is fixed, so the result is reproducible.\n\nThe method contains nothing specific to these stores, waves or dates. The stores the gate covers are defined as those in waves with no store live yet in the extract.\n\n## Why the earlier figures should not go to the Committee\n- **Programme readout (basket size +5.5%):** it measures the wrong thing. Basket size rises when shoppers combine trips, even when sales stay flat. It also uses planned rather than actual go-live dates, and averages the uplift over all post-go-live weeks instead of the run-rate weeks.\n- **FP&A notebook (net sales +6.7%):** it reran the same flawed model on net sales. That model:\n  - treats any week with transactions as comparable, so install-closure weeks are included;\n  - uses planned go-live dates;\n  - assumes one chain-wide trend that the formats do not share. This error alone inflates the estimates substantially, for example wave 1 comes out at about +9% instead of +4.1%.\n- **Both figures answer the wrong question.** They describe the live stores, which are mostly full-kit Supercentre and Market stores. The gate asks about waves 5–6 as planned, which are mostly compact.\n\n## What changed in the package\n- `panel.py`: go-live dates from the install log, and comparable weeks from the closures table.\n- `estimate.py`: the new estimation method, documented at the top of the file.\n- `report.py` and `cli.py`: write the contract outputs and print the gate figure.\n- Release 3.0.0 is noted in `RELEASES.md`.\n\n## Caveats for the Committee\n- **Possible paths forward:** the evidence suggests a full-kit-only tranche would clear the hurdle. That is a different proposal from the gate as approved and would need its own business case.\n- **Sister-store effects not checked:** the extract has no store locations, so I could not test the approval assumption that sales lost by one store don't go to another nearby Meridian store."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":594,"cache_read_input_tokens":49756,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":594,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":4,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"global"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"687223dc-a54c-4d3b-baa1-e8651351f08d","uuid":"a67ef2b3-4e85-4494-98ea-391efdd7b948","timestamp":"2026-10-01T01:07:20.299Z","request_id":"req_011CfaczbxrdLyKvuFgUoC9W"}

That is a different proposal from the gate as approved and would need its own business case.\n- **Sister-store effects not checked:** the extract has no store locations, so I could not test the approval assumption that sales lost by one store don't go to another nearby Meridian store.","ttft_ms":2470,"type":"result","duration_ms":303949,"uuid":"bb770af4-1f79-44e4-97f0-a7b0668e0218","ttft_stream_ms":2357,"time_to_request_ms":94,"first_content_frame_ms":2358,"queued_turn_count":0,"result_index":0}
